- **Next action:** add adversarial dense-feed cases for cheapest-fare preservation and feasible transfer chains, then improve candidate selection within the same bounded memory contract. Compare candidate counts, returned route chains, time and peak memory; keep the visible partial result until relevant-candidate completeness is established.

### GTFS bounded-sample quality — 2026-10-19

- On Server branch `codex/journey-search-completeness`, changed graph-cap retention from a feed-order prefix to a deterministic per-route priority reservoir. Memory remains bounded by the same proportional graph ceiling; `candidateGraphTruncated` and user-facing partial-search signaling remain intact.
- Added a regression with 5,001 dense trips and a faster valid trip placed last in feed insertion order. The route-level bounded search retains and returns the late fast trip. Focused GTFS tests pass 38/38; full unit suite passes 235, 0 fail, 3 opt-in skips; `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, and `git diff --check` pass locally.
- Exact-feed replay uses the cached Lviv static schedule (72 routes, 15,716 trips, 1,071 stops), not realtime vehicle positions. With a 512 MiB Node heap limit the search returned 100 results and 35 distinct route chains (prior recorded prefix-cap sample: 21), reported graph/search/result truncation, completed in about 3 seconds, and measured ~361 MiB heap / ~548 MiB RSS. This is a single-query diagnostic, not concurrency or staging acceptance.
- Follow-up isolated-process replays at 07:00, 10:00 and 13:00 Kyiv time each returned 100 options, with 43, 37 and 41 distinct route chains respectively; all clearly reported graph/search/result truncation. Post-query heap was 315–320 MiB; process RSS readings varied 274–497 MiB across separate runs and are not load-test percentiles.
- Added opt-in debug counts to locate graph truncation. On the four-hour Lviv query, 68 of 72 selected routes contributed graph candidates; 40 routes reached the 1,388-segment per-route limit. The retained graph had 69,575 segments. This confirms the partial result is driven by dense per-route truncation, not the nominal 100,000 global ceiling.
- A controlled local experiment temporarily raised only the global ceiling to 150,000 (per-route limit remains 5,000). The same query retained 39 distinct chains instead of 35 and used ~401 MiB post-query heap / ~565 MiB RSS; it still reported truncation. The limit was restored to 100,000 because the diversity gain is modest and no multi-request/load evidence establishes the extra memory margin as safe.
- Commit `69be55f32c595c545757b4341c2247bf025d842c` passed exact-head push/PR CI `39241040483` and `39241045903`; diagnostics commit `5d6ad9cf28da15d8f56614fde1b1fdbd3a17c0dc` passed exact-head push CI `39241725345` and PR CI `39241732394`. All workflows passed `npm audit`, lint, typecheck, unit suite, migrations, and PostgreSQL/Redis integration. Only non-failing GitHub action/runtime deprecation and runner-image migration warnings were reported.
- **Next action:** preserve the 100,000 default; use the new debug counters to design a more efficient complete candidate-graph strategy before raising limits. Continue reducing per-request memory and broadening feasibility/ranking acceptance; validate against reference itineraries and keep truncation visible until search completeness is proven or a validated completeness contract is defined.

### Lviv GTFS through the authenticated Journey API — 2026-10-19

- Started the exact pinned Server SHA `7c459ded4f96b106f85aa2e76bfd9440e3de6293` on `127.0.0.1:3310`, with `NODE_ENV=development`, development OTP enabled, auth bypass disabled, and the isolated local `marshgo_e2e` PostgreSQL/PostGIS database plus loopback Redis. The temporary API was stopped after the probe; port 3310 is closed. No staging or production system was touched.
- The isolated DB's Lviv GTFS provider previously had no validated report or bbox, so the authenticated API correctly returned zero journeys and marked bus/tram/trolleybus unsupported. Ran the candidate's real `testProviderConnection` validator against the catalog's open Lviv static GTFS source, then enabled only that provider record inside `marshgo_e2e`. Validation passed: 1,071 stops, 72 routes (64 bus, 8 tram), 15,716 trips, 426,685 stop times; feed bbox covers Lviv. This proves the feed and ingestion validation path, not live vehicle availability.
- Using the OTP request/verify flow and bearer token (not the development auth bypass), called `/api/v1/journeys/search` for Malo Holosko → Sykhiv at 07:00 Kyiv time on 2026-10-20 with bus/tram selected. `BALANCED`, `FASTEST`, and `CHEAPEST` each returned HTTP 200 and two actual scheduled GTFS candidates. `FASTEST` ranked a 64-minute/46-UAH route first; `CHEAPEST` ranked a 69-minute/23-UAH route first; `BALANCED` retained both trade-offs. The 23-UAH option used A37 at 07:05–07:40 then Tr24 at 07:43–08:08; its second leg is covered by the validated Lviv 40-minute LeoCard transfer rule, not an unexplained zero fare.
- The API correctly marks all three searches `partial` and both candidate-generation and result sampling truncated. `CHEAPEST` also reports unpriced candidates as unsupported, so those results cannot be represented as exhaustive cheapest options. Other requested categories are explicitly blocked when the corridor lacks a routed provider.
- `WALKING_ROUTING_URL` is absent. Walk access/egress legs are stored as `straight-line-estimate`; Site renders them as approximate/dashed and labels the map accordingly. This is not verified pedestrian geometry. Live vehicle positions were not part of this scheduled route test.
- Exact candidate unit tests `journey-scoring.test.ts` and `journey-planner.test.ts`: 24 passed, 0 failed. The API probe is manual local integration evidence, not staging/production acceptance. No production candidate code changed in this step; only the isolated test DB provider validation state changed.
- **Next action:** continue route completeness and verified foot-routing work; keep city live-tracking, staging, and production acceptance open.

### API-level strategy ranking regression — 2026-10-19

### GTFS candidate fare ranking correction — 2026-10-19

- Revalidated Server PR #10 (`codex/journey-search-completeness`) at exact prior head `4a4e4ef10920851c7808042333bc5ac7ee5b85ab`; it was open and both exact-SHA hosted checks passed. Base PR #9 remains open at `7c459ded4f96b106f85aa2e76bfd9440e3de6293` with both checks passing.
- Found that GTFS's internal balanced candidate sampler assigned a zero price penalty when a candidate's fare was unknown. The final cross-provider scorer already treats unknown fares as incomparable/worst for price; the earlier sampler could nevertheless elevate an unpriced option into a limited candidate pool as if it were free.
- Added `rankGtfsItinerariesForCandidateSampling`: when at least one comparable UAH fare exists, unknown-fare candidates get the maximum price cost for this sampling objective, while remaining eligible under time, transfer, and walking objectives. With no comparable fares, sampling leaves the price dimension neutral. Added a regression test that distinguishes a verified zero-UAH fare from an unknown fare.
- Commit `95ec90d3e29b73e704cbac0d2faf2412a1019457` was pushed fast-forward to the existing PR #10 branch; PR stays open and unmerged. Targeted GTFS tests 36/36; full unit suite 233 passed, 0 failed, 3 opt-in skips; typecheck, lint, and `git diff --check` pass. Isolated Postgres/PostGIS + Redis integration completed 30/30, including route search, booking, rendezvous, persistence/restart, tenant checks, and realtime. These are local evidence, not staging/production evidence. Exact-SHA hosted run `39234971756` is still pending.
- The behavioral output from the balanced *sampling* pool changed only on mixed known/unknown-fare candidate sets; public displayed fare remains unchanged (`null` stays unknown), and the final strategy scorer is unchanged.

### Bounded GTFS graph memory and OOM reproduction — 2026-10-19

- Current Server branch `codex/journey-search-completeness`, PR #10, commit `6fdfd56fe9c036454af6e306892961a9a2a6dc71` is pushed. Reverse transfer-node indexing now stores de-duplicated references in arrays rather than per-stop Sets.
- A deterministic regression with 5,001 direct trips proves the candidate graph is bounded while valid itinerary results survive and partial status is explicit. Local full unit: 235 passed, 0 failed, 3 optional skips; typecheck, lint, focused GTFS tests (38/38) and diff check passed. Exact-head hosted Server CI `39240147998` and `39240153470` both passed, including migration and Postgres/Redis integration jobs.
- On the same locally cached Lviv GTFS archive and same query, the pre-cap version built 1,104,017 segments and ended in V8 heap OOM after >2 GiB heap use. The capped version built 83,475 segments, completed in ~1.63 s, and peaked at ~681 MB RSS. It returned 100 routes and correctly surfaced graph, stop-selection, and candidate-sampling truncation. This fixes the reproduced single-request OOM but retains materially high memory and partial results; no concurrency load or production memory acceptance is claimed. With Node limited to a 512 MiB old-space heap, the same query still completed in ~3.82 s at ~501 MB RSS, returned 100 routes, and preserved all truncation flags. Top 100 included 21 distinct route chains; this supports diversity for this query only, not exhaustive coverage.
- A repeat local full integration run could not connect to PostgreSQL at `127.0.0.1:5434`; Docker CLI also hung because its daemon was unavailable. This run is NOT_TESTED, not a pass. A preceding integration run on the prior SHA passed 30/30, but does not cover the current change. The exact-head hosted CI will be the current migration/Postgres/Redis evidence.
- **Next action:** continue reducing cache/graph memory and improving route/time coverage without suppressing truncation, then refresh the release pins. Keep production `NOT_RELEASED`.

### GTFS candidate-budget experiment — 2026-10-19

- On the exact Server `d249df1…` source, enabled internal `MARSHGO_GTFS_SEARCH_DEBUG=1` for the same style of Lviv north→Sykhiv static-feed request. Baseline with the committed 100,000 total / route-proportional cap: 15,716 trips considered, 70,627 direct segments retained, 41/72 candidate routes at their 1,388 segment cap, 1,561 feasible first legs, 4,391 expansion steps and 250 ranked candidate results. The two service-date query window ran in about 817 ms after feed acquisition; `stopSelectionTruncated`, `segmentGraphTruncated`, and result sampling all remained true. RSS at graph construction was ~578 MB in this process.
- Temporarily raised only the total segment cap to 160,000 in the isolated Server checkout and replayed with a 512 MiB V8 old-space cap. It retained 100,514 segments, reduced routes at per-route cap from 41 to 33, increased feasible first legs from 1,561 to 2,286, and took about 1,138 ms through ranking. The query still returned its 250-candidate internal ceiling, still had stop-selection/graph/result-sampling truncation, and still returned only 10 requested itineraries. The one-process RSS/heap samples varied by allocator/GC and do not establish safe concurrency capacity.
- **Decision:** reverted the experimental 160k constant; no production source change or commit came from this experiment. It improves intermediate candidate breadth but does not close the result-completeness gate and lacks a safe multi-request memory benchmark. Keep the verified 100k bound until candidate ranking and bounded memory are validated together.
- **Next action:** improve the candidate selection strategy within the current memory ceiling, make sure the exposed partial-search warning is verified in browser behavior, then profile at a small isolated concurrency before considering any budget change.

### GTFS graph demand refinement for realtime schedule window — 2026-10-19

### Live Lviv feed exposed and fixed cached GTFS metadata loss — 2026-10-19

- A bounded live probe used the Server safe-fetch/parser against the public Lviv static GTFS, VehiclePositions and TripUpdates endpoints. HTTP responses were 200. GTFS-RT VehiclePositions decoded to 610 entries with a 4-second feed timestamp age; TripUpdates decoded to 718 updates with a 13-second timestamp age. A second Server vehicle parse observed 609 entries, of which 566 passed the per-vehicle 90-second freshness rule. Probe counts vary by request time and are not an SLA.
- The probe exposed a real issue in `server/mobility/transportLayers.ts`: the cache Zod schema stripped `directionId` and `geometrySource`. Because the live Journey builder only trusts exact `gtfs_shape` geometry, cached Lviv route geometry was dropped despite the upstream GTFS containing it. Fixed the cache schema and added a regression test that feeds the cached-schema output into the live segment builder.
- Post-fix public feed replay parsed 144 directional geometries and 565 fresh vehicles; multiple exact routes had both direction geometries and matching live vehicles (for example Lviv route А51: two 160-point directions and 20 fresh route positions, all with identified trips and stop progress). This is an external snapshot probe, not an authenticated product/staging acceptance.
- Rechecks on the fix: focused live-segment/vehicle suite 14/14 PASS; `npm run typecheck` PASS; `npm run lint` PASS; `git diff --check` PASS. Commit `4c7a7a9597cc4e702089f3320f0676a18ae8ce2b` was pushed by fast-forward to `codex/journey-search-completeness`, updating open Server PR #10. No main merge or deployment. Exact-head CI runs `39286050214` and `39286063561` are queued; prior green checks at `6550df3` do not substitute.
- Updated the acceptance matrix with the cache defect and new evidence. The release manifest remains pinned to the last fully materialized candidate until exact-head CI completes; release remains `NOT_RELEASED`.
- **Next action:** run the complete Server unit suite on the fix SHA while CI queues; then refresh exact-head CI. If green, rematerialize and move on to authenticated end-to-end/staging gates.

### Prevent stale Redis payloads from surviving route metadata fix — 2026-10-19

- Follow-up review found that changing the Zod schema alone would still accept an old Redis payload that had already lost `directionId` and `geometrySource`; those optional fields would remain absent until cache expiry. Added `schemaVersion: 2` to both GTFS and GeoJSON network cache parameters so the old shared key is bypassed.
- Added an explicit regression asserting that the previous and current shared GTFS network cache keys differ. Focused map/transit suite: 28/28 pass; typecheck, lint and `git diff --check` pass.
- Commit `ff6a3e2` (`fix(cache): version GTFS network payload schema`) pushed by normal fast-forward to the existing `codex/journey-search-completeness` PR #10. No force-push, merge or deployment.
- **Next action:** refresh exact-head PR CI for `ff6a3e2`; after hosted checks, update release materialization to this SHA and continue authenticated API/staging gates.

- Replayed the same public Lviv static-schedule query with `MARSHGO_GTFS_SEARCH_DEBUG=1` on Server `4ac8081315f0183d7778b03218991c67418a6adc`.
- Observed stages: 27 origin / 29 destination stops selected; 466 corridor stops / 813 interchange stops; 96,819 retained segments from a 100,000 graph budget across 71 candidate routes, with 3 routes at their 5,000-segment cap; 3,746 first legs; 45,021 search steps; 509 ranked candidates against a 500 candidate pool; 20 returned. The API correctly reports search and result truncation.
- There are distinct bounded-search causes: stop selection discarded additional nearby stops; route-segment graph hit its global/per-route caps; candidate pool and objective reservoir reached their limits. Do not conflate this with feed unavailability or say the result set is exhaustive.
- Reviewed `journeyEnd = earliestDeparture + maximumJourneySeconds`: final itinerary duration includes the initial wait from `earliestDeparture`, so a transit leg beyond that bound cannot satisfy the current maximum total-duration contract. The apparent late-window discrepancy is not changed absent an explicit contract change.
- Memory observation for the `tsx` read-only probe was about 618 MB RSS (about 384 MB V8 heap) after loading the feed and query graph; it completed in roughly 1.14 seconds. This is a single local diagnostic, not a production capacity benchmark.
- **Next action:** add focused, deterministic coverage for each exposed truncation cause and assess a lower-allocation candidate representation before adjusting any cap. Preserve truthful partial metadata.

### Current dirty Server real-feed profile — 2026-10-19

- Replayed the read-only public Lviv static GTFS search on the currently available dirty Server checkout (`feature/transport-layer-availability`, HEAD `182c77ae4f78d576a287007d8e0864fb130f43f8`; working tree has 23 modified/untracked paths). - Feed contained 1,071 stops, 72 routes, 15,716 trips and 426,685 stop-time rows. Search built 430,040 scheduled candidate segments, selected 20 origin and 20 destination stops, and returned 100 itineraries / 322 displayed segments in 1.33 seconds. The candidate search is **not exhaustive**: it reports `searchTruncated=true` and `resultLimitReached=true`; internal diagnostic counters show 9,727 search steps, 800 ranked candidates (200 for each 2–5-leg chain), and both per-depth candidate sampling and a per-depth search budget were reached. RSS at completion was about 721 MiB in this single local process.
- A second isolated process separated load from query: parsed-feed baseline was 518–522 MiB RSS / 236–242 MiB heap; after search it was 679–737 MiB RSS / 345–505 MiB heap. Explicit GC reduced heap to 128 MiB, while RSS stayed around 674 MiB. This supports both a large in-memory timetable baseline and substantial temporary graph allocations; it does not establish container peak or concurrent capacity.
- The previous exact-candidate Server measurement (`4ac8081`) had a different branch/revision and returned a 20-item page; its 96,819 segment / 618 MiB figures are not substituted for this current dirty checkout. Neither measurement is a staging or production capacity result.
- This confirms a material capacity/completeness risk: candidate materialization is large and the API correctly labels partial search. No arbitrary cap was raised and no result was described as complete. Keep the current bounded search open for a memory-conscious graph representation and repeatable candidate-recall validation.
- Docker Desktop remains stopped; the local test PostGIS and Redis containers are therefore unavailable for browser/API integration. They are test dependencies only, not the deployed product. No containers were started or reconfigured.
- **Next action:** add controlled deterministic coverage around candidate truncation/reporting, then profile a compact/lazy segment representation against the same actual-feed case and existing routing regressions. Do not merge or release while the search silently omits candidates; preserve explicit partial status.

### Candidate-pool completeness signal correction — 2026-10-19

- In the dirty Server checkout, the internal candidate cap could stop accepting additional feasible paths while the returned `searchTruncated` field stayed false. The public search then could not distinguish a complete ranked page from an incomplete candidate-generation pass.
- Added a deterministic three-trip fixture at a one-result request limit. First run reproduced the defect (candidate cap reached, `resultLimitReached=true`, but `searchTruncated=false`). Updated the search to mark truncation only when an additional path is actually rejected by the candidate/route-chain cap; reaching the cap exactly with no omitted path is not a false positive. `searchTruncated` now includes confirmed candidate-sampling omissions.
- Focused regressions: **2/2 PASS**, including the adjacent ordinary result-page case (still `searchTruncated=false`). Replayed the Lviv public feed after the correction: 100 results in 1.41 s, 9,731 search steps, 800 retained candidates, ~717 MiB RSS; `searchTruncated=true` remains truthful. The search was not made exhaustive and its result count did not regress to the overly early 11-item intermediate experiment.
- Full current Server unit suite: **219 PASS / 0 FAIL / 1 opt-in integration SKIP**; TypeScript typecheck PASS; ESLint PASS. Docker/PostGIS/Redis integrations were not run because the Docker daemon is stopped.
- Reduced repeated GTFS time conversion by reusing scheduled arrival/departure `Date` values for candidate segments sharing a trip stop-time. GTFS timetable and realtime routing tests: **41/41 PASS**. A one-run actual-feed sample remained about 1.42 s and ~668 MiB RSS before GC; this is within observed run-to-run variance, so no measurable performance gain is claimed yet. The major memory work remains the candidate segment graph itself.
- A separate post-load GC sample on the same feed showed 526 MiB RSS / 236 MiB heap immediately after parse and 514 MiB RSS / 127 MiB heap after GC. The retained parsed timetable is therefore about 127 MiB of JS heap in this run; process RSS also includes allocator/native memory that GC does not return. The post-search heap returned to about 128 MiB after GC. These are single-process diagnostics, not production capacity evidence.
- Measured API event-loop responsiveness during the same synchronous search with a 50 ms interval: the search took 1,321 ms and the largest timer delay was also 1,321 ms. This confirms the route search blocks other work on that Node event loop for roughly the whole query. No worker-thread or cooperative-yield mechanism exists in the current Server code. This is a production availability/latency blocker, not just a memory concern.
- Tested the naïve `Worker({ workerData: parsedFeed })` approach as a bounded architecture probe. Cloning/spawning took 292 ms, the parent event-loop timer still stalled by 243 ms, and process RSS reached ~911 MiB before the worker failed to resolve the current extensionless TS import. No search ran and this is **not** accepted as an implementation. It rules out cloning the full feed into a new worker per request. Any CPU offload must keep a worker-owned feed cache (without also loading every schedule in the API process) or use a bounded cooperative algorithm; runtime loading and memory need measured verification.
- Tested a worker-owned feed path: a persistent-style Worker loaded the public feed itself through the repository’s `tsx/esm/api` runtime hook, parsed it in the worker, ran the Lviv query and returned 100 itineraries with the same `searchTruncated=true`. Worker search took 1,348 ms while the parent’s 50 ms timer observed only 2 ms maximum delay; total process RSS was ~710 MiB and the worker heap ~375 MiB. This is a successful architecture probe, not production code: it bypassed the API provider query, used one feed/one search, and did not test warm cache, queueing, cancellation, secondary transit-composition queries or staging.
- Endpoint dependency scan found the feed groups are also used for metro + micromobility station chains, reverse carpool + transit composition, and supported-mode reporting. Moving only the first results search to a Worker would leave duplicate parent-side feed parsing and synchronous work. A real integration must route all three query families and feed-derived capabilities through the same worker-owned cache before deleting parent feed loads.
- Changes are uncommitted on the pre-existing dirty branch; no commit, push, merge or deployment occurred. `git diff --check` is recorded separately after the final review.
- **Next action:** profile and reduce candidate-graph object allocation under deterministic equivalence tests. Do not claim production readiness while the real-feed search remains partial or the staging/browser gates remain open.

### GTFS graph-budget allocation follow-up — 2026-10-20

- In the isolated Server PR #12 worktree, reviewed the bounded graph allocator after a real two-source Lviv+Stryi static-feed probe hit the 100k graph budget and 1M search-step cap.
- Found that the allocator could assign a route more candidate slots than its estimated eligible stop-pair supply. ### Exact dense-feed comparison after queue-scheduler change — 2026-10-20

- Replayed the preserved two-source Lviv+Stryi worker probe against baseline Server SHA `4a9015c476cc8ead44617c4544456a5cbf1a8f06` and PR #13 SHA `f235ca91c75996bd2d5645642780ba7467a7574c`, using the same feeds, coordinates, 30-minute departure window, mode and transfer limits.
- Candidate work was identical on both revisions: 97,413 graph segments; 346 first legs; 1,000,000 search steps; 1,651 retained candidates (506 two-leg, 529 three-leg, 556 four-leg); 100 returned; graph, stop selection, candidate sampling and step budget all still report partial/truncated. Peak queued work was 38,470; PR #13 removed 22 queues after they drained.
- PR #13 first/warm wall time was 10.0/6.7 s; baseline was 10.4/6.7 s. This difference is within run variance and is not a demonstrated performance improvement. Worker RSS varied materially between runs (PR #13 diagnostics about 849/942 MiB; baseline about 700/903 MiB), so neither run is a memory-capacity guarantee. Parent event-loop lag was 5 ms in both.
- Also reran the two daytime single-Lviv route cases at the exact query inputs from the saved probe: 10 results each; 23,096 and 20,916 search steps. Both remained partial because graph/nearby-stop/result sampling limits were reached, despite no global search-step ceiling. This confirms the earlier ~159k/1m measurements belong to different query inputs and should not be mixed.
- **Interpretation:** queue cleanup is correctness-neutral but did not materially advance the measured dense query. The main gap remains graph truncation and high multi-leg candidate expansion. Closed PR #13 after the identical comparison; its remote branch is retained and was not deleted. Do not count the experiment as solving the routing-coverage requirement.
- **Next action:** inspect why whole-trip route retention exhausts per-route slots and why 4-leg expansion consumes the global work budget. Develop a targeted bounded candidate representation that preserves pickup/drop-off stops and exact transfer feasibility, then prove it against dense-feed regression fixtures before changing route or graph caps.

### GTFS transfer-rule retention correction (2026-10-20)

- Continued from the bounded-search Lviv comparison and inspected the current Server PR #17 worktree at `/private/tmp/MarshGO-Server-rendezvous-stack-20261020`.
- Found an adversarial correctness gap: temporal reachability alone could prioritize a middle trip explicitly forbidden by a trip-specific GTFS transfer rule, allowing it to crowd out a feasible alternative under tight graph bounds.
- Updated `server/journey/gtfsTimetable.ts` to preserve trip/route/stop identity in retention events and apply GTFS rule specificity/ambiguity before treating a connection as a positive retention signal. ### Відхилення event-label v2 на frozen Lviv A/B — 2026-10-23

- Ізольований test-only lazy label search пройшов малий oracle і локальні gates: GTFS **72/72**, typecheck, ESLint, diff-check PASS. Replay виконано рівно один раз після цього gate на архіві Lviv GTFS SHA-256 `bb4ac0ce9127b4967ad889379f523b04b84a0bb033814aa8c844526b42078164`, Node `v25.4.0` (не pinned `24.21.0`), трьох раніше зафіксованих OD і незмінених параметрах запиту.
- На кожному OD baseline мав 100 результатів і flags `searchTruncated=true` та `candidateGraphTruncated=true`; prototype уперся у власний ліміт 100,000 expanded labels. Baseline-vs-prototype ID recall **0/100 на кожному OD**; усі переможці FASTEST/CHEAPEST/BALANCED відсутні (**0/3 на кожному OD**). Prototype тримав 66–4,581 path identities, матеріалізував 68,166–86,055 legs, працював 5.3–5.6 с проти baseline 1.6–2.7 с; final heap 189–418 MB, RSS 483–547 MB. Лічильники search різних алгоритмів не тотожні, але втрата всіх baseline IDs/winners є достатньою підставою для відхилення.
- Root cause: coordinate-shaped adapter відтворив stop shed і exact transfer decision, але не production corridor/catchment та `interchangeStopIds` candidate-graph eligibility і mode/type filters. Отже, алгоритми шукали по різних графах. Малий fixture parity не доводить parity на щільному фіді.
- Результат: **v2 відхилено; не підключався до production, не комічений і не push-нутий.** Ізольований Server worktree лишився на базовому SHA `b65882584a3ad1e8dd7512d13176f52299cded51`; PR #31, budgets і release pins не змінені.
- **Наступна контрольована спроба:** тільки behavior-preserving спільний production candidate-graph context, який передається обом алгоритмам; спочатку довести точну рівність candidate graph/first-leg IDs на fixtures. Якщо точний shared graph не вдається винести без зміни семантики — зупинити цей алгоритмічний напрям. Новий frozen replay не повторювати на іншій множині кандидатів.
